Selected: H-P4YGE2
Design: Commit 4047c01 on branch feat/one-subject-per-run, from 03e9e3d. Applies the owner's return of R-9SMRB2#4: ADR 0020 writes a run subject as {base, patch, files}, as the code, replay-spec and glossary do. The code (cmd/, internal/) is unchanged since b80d95d (git diff b80d95d 4047c01 -- cmd internal is empty), whose removal checks R-P1M8A8 logged.

Limits:

- R-0HABWN#1 closed by R-CX1G1C (H-P65RW9 (one-subject-per-arm) supports) — F1: a sandbox record with two --patch Arms at base B is kept as one run subject {B, [pa, pb]}, so a Hypothesis tied to B plus Arm a's patch is never covered though that Arm ran at B; a subject has at most one patch.
- R-0HABWN#2 accepted by owner-dat — F2: line 3's new tests fail to build on 9429fc1, so neither half fails on the commit before its change with a message naming its rule (line 9's case for line 3).

- R-0HABWN#4 closed by R-CX1G1C (H-P65RW9 (one-subject-per-arm) supports) — F4: a Hypothesis tied with --base B and two paths of the same bytes is never held, because run files are unique by sha256 and subject files unique by path.
- R-0HABWN#5 closed by R-CX1G1C (H-P65RW9 (one-subject-per-arm) supports) — F5: hyp subject --base exits 2 when git is not on PATH; a missing tool is an environment problem, exit 3.

- R-9SMRB2#5 accepted by owner-dat — F5: a Result written by a 7257753 or 3ec4979 binary keeps an Arm's patch inside files, so 0cda2f8 replays it as a run at B given input P, which covers bare B.

# G-1391DM hyp builds the store layer every layout needs — complete

Part of #138; the layout, one view over every store, is #168, which builds on this. The candidate is a hyp commit; go test on its base and on the candidate is the instrument. The texts ride on this Goal as a prose-only change (PR #165): skills/hyp-prototype step 2 and README.md say to run hyp init where the repository's agent instructions say trees live, or at the repository root when they name none; AGENTS.md says a new Goal goes in the store that holds the Goal it continues, otherwise in a new investigations/<topic>/, and that a PR whose events file conflicts on GitHub is merged locally first, taking main's .tree/.gitattributes before git merge origin/main. The spec changes in the same PR: replay-spec §1, §2 and §6, sandbox-spec, and an ADR amending 0020. Out of this Goal: a patch naming a path under .tree/ together with --store; a hyp check warning for a store whose .gitattributes lacks the union line; an older pinned hyp reading a live union-merged store, which is accepted and stated in the ADR; and a store below a base's root, which #168 covers.

May change: hyp in this repository (cmd/, internal/, docs/), as commits on one local branch pushed as a PR the run never merges
May change: the hyp init placement texts in skills/hyp-prototype/SKILL.md, README.md and AGENTS.md, and any other text in skills/, README.md or AGENTS.md the change makes wrong
May change: a .tree/.gitattributes in each store under investigations/, and the events and evidence of this Goal's store, investigations/checkpoint-store
Read only: the events of every other store under investigations/; every frozen recipe version under recipes/
Never: run hyp init at the repository root
Never: replace the installed hyp binary at ~/.local/bin/hyp
Never: merge the PR

## Q-75N8YM Which hyp commit implements the shared store layer? — answered

The candidate is a hyp commit. Every mechanism the lines name can sit in one candidate, so no two options pass the difference test: the run adds each candidate commit as a Hypothesis, with the base as its rival.

Selected: H-VG5D0F
Design: Commit d966e85 on branch feat/shared-store-layer, from 07d44c0. Its code is 07d44c0's; TestAppendAfterRejectedIDKeepsClock now writes the rejected id 0zzz..., which sorts after every id until 3084 (review finding 2 of R-SJXBFW), and replay-spec rule 1 and the ValidID comment say the id form is 26 upper-case Crockford base32 characters, which admits an id above the greatest ULID (finding 3). Written and vetted only, run nowhere before E's rubric.

## Q-PC04ED Which hyp commit implements the shared-store design? — answered

Selected: H-P4YGE2
Design: Commit 4047c01 on branch feat/one-subject-per-run, from 03e9e3d. Applies the owner's return of R-9SMRB2#4: ADR 0020 writes a run subject as {base, patch, files}, as the code, replay-spec and glossary do. The code (cmd/, internal/) is unchanged since b80d95d (git diff b80d95d 4047c01 -- cmd internal is empty), whose removal checks R-P1M8A8 logged.

Limits:

- R-0HABWN#1 closed by R-CX1G1C (H-P65RW9 (one-subject-per-arm) supports) — F1: a sandbox record with two --patch Arms at base B is kept as one run subject {B, [pa, pb]}, so a Hypothesis tied to B plus Arm a's patch is never covered though that Arm ran at B; a subject has at most one patch.
- R-0HABWN#2 accepted by owner-dat — F2: line 3's new tests fail to build on 9429fc1, so neither half fails on the commit before its change with a message naming its rule (line 9's case for line 3).
- R-0HABWN#3 closed by R-CX1G1C (H-P65RW9 (one-subject-per-arm) supports) — F3: no test runs rule 26 on a base: a bare-B subject re-tied after a run at B plus a patch must be admitted, and refused with 'subject ran in' after a run at exactly B.
- R-0HABWN#4 closed by R-CX1G1C (H-P65RW9 (one-subject-per-arm) supports) — F4: a Hypothesis tied with --base B and two paths of the same bytes is never held, because run files are unique by sha256 and subject files unique by path.
- R-0HABWN#5 closed by R-CX1G1C (H-P65RW9 (one-subject-per-arm) supports) — F5: hyp subject --base exits 2 when git is not on PATH; a missing tool is an environment problem, exit 3.
- R-0HABWN#6 closed by R-CX1G1C (H-P65RW9 (one-subject-per-arm) supports) — F6: replay admits a Result whose subjects are not empty while its evidence is empty, though subjects are copied only from cited records.
- R-0HABWN#7 closed by R-7XVMDC (H-W35076 supports) — Q: a sandbox run at B with an --inputs file never covers a Hypothesis whose subject is bare B, because a based subject is held only by a run given exactly its files.
- R-9SMRB2#1 closed by R-Q9WTZB (H-JA5S86 contradicts), R-KBW141 (H-AX7M2K supports) — F1: a Hypothesis tied to B plus arm.patch is covered by a run at bare B with no Arm whose --inputs file has the patch's bytes, though the patch never changed the code.
- R-9SMRB2#2 closed by R-Q9WTZB (H-JA5S86 contradicts), R-KBW141 (H-AX7M2K supports) — F2: a Hypothesis tied to B plus an input file I is held by a run at B whose patch is I, because a Hypothesis's subject does not say which of its files is the patch.
- R-9SMRB2#3 closed by R-Q9WTZB (H-JA5S86 contradicts), R-KBW141 (H-AX7M2K supports) — F3: replay-spec rule 27 still names 'a subject with no base and no files' and not the new 'no base, no patch and no files' or 'patch ... is not a sha256' rejections.
- R-9SMRB2#4 closed by R-RRYBPH (H-P4YGE2 supports) — F4: ADR 0020 still writes a run subject as {base, files} with no patch field, while the code, replay-spec and glossary write {base, patch, files}.
- R-9SMRB2#5 accepted by owner-dat — F5: a Result written by a 7257753 or 3ec4979 binary keeps an Arm's patch inside files, so 0cda2f8 replays it as a run at B given input P, which covers bare B.
- R-9SMRB2#6 closed by R-Q9WTZB (H-JA5S86 contradicts), R-KBW141 (H-AX7M2K supports) — F6: no test runs rule 26 after the owner's case: once a run at B given inputs holds a bare-B Hypothesis, a new tie of that Hypothesis must be refused with 'subject ran in'.
- R-9SMRB2#7 accepted by owner-dat — Q: a sandbox with Arms keeps only patched subjects, so a bare-B control its Launch builds gives no bare-B subject, and a bare-B Hypothesis stays uncovered by it.
- R-3EN4FB#1 closed by R-P1M8A8 (H-71PQR7 supports) — G1: a sandbox.json Arm whose sha256 is not lowercase hex is kept as a run at bare B with no patch, so that patched run covers a Hypothesis whose subject is bare B.

# G-1391DM hyp builds the store layer every layout needs — complete

Part of #138; the layout, one view over every store, is #168, which builds on this. The candidate is a hyp commit; go test on its base and on the candidate is the instrument. The texts ride on this Goal as a prose-only change (PR #165): skills/hyp-prototype step 2 and README.md say to run hyp init where the repository's agent instructions say trees live, or at the repository root when they name none; AGENTS.md says a new Goal goes in the store that holds the Goal it continues, otherwise in a new investigations/<topic>/, and that a PR whose events file conflicts on GitHub is merged locally first, taking main's .tree/.gitattributes before git merge origin/main. The spec changes in the same PR: replay-spec §1, §2 and §6, sandbox-spec, and an ADR amending 0020. Out of this Goal: a patch naming a path under .tree/ together with --store; a hyp check warning for a store whose .gitattributes lacks the union line; an older pinned hyp reading a live union-merged store, which is accepted and stated in the ADR; and a store below a base's root, which #168 covers.

May change: hyp in this repository (cmd/, internal/, docs/), as commits on one local branch pushed as a PR the run never merges
May change: the hyp init placement texts in skills/hyp-prototype/SKILL.md, README.md and AGENTS.md, and any other text in skills/, README.md or AGENTS.md the change makes wrong
May change: a .tree/.gitattributes in each store under investigations/, and the events and evidence of this Goal's store, investigations/checkpoint-store
Read only: the events of every other store under investigations/; every frozen recipe version under recipes/
Never: run hyp init at the repository root
Never: replace the installed hyp binary at ~/.local/bin/hyp
Never: merge the PR

## Q-75N8YM Which hyp commit implements the shared store layer? — answered

The candidate is a hyp commit. Every mechanism the lines name can sit in one candidate, so no two options pass the difference test: the run adds each candidate commit as a Hypothesis, with the base as its rival.

Selected: H-VG5D0F
Design: Commit d966e85 on branch feat/shared-store-layer, from 07d44c0. Its code is 07d44c0's; TestAppendAfterRejectedIDKeepsClock now writes the rejected id 0zzz..., which sorts after every id until 3084 (review finding 2 of R-SJXBFW), and replay-spec rule 1 and the ValidID comment say the id form is 26 upper-case Crockford base32 characters, which admits an id above the greatest ULID (finding 3). Written and vetted only, run nowhere before E's rubric.

Limits:

- R-ZN1VJJ#1 closed by R-SXCDZW (H-VB4XPK (store-layer-2) supports) — Line 4: two clones whose clock reads earlier than the newest id each append one event under the same actor; both get id last+1, and after a union merge which event replay keeps depends on the line order in the file.
- R-ZN1VJJ#2 accepted by owner-dat — Line 4: an events file holds a line that does not parse; the same lines in another order make hyp check and hyp status name another file:line for it.
- R-SXCDZW#1 closed by R-RZMF87 (H-J7N18Y (store-layer-3) supports) — Line 4 and line 1: one events file holds two lines with one id whose actor fields differ; the store cut keeps one line and hyp status --as-of applies the other.
- R-SXCDZW#2 closed by R-RZMF87 (H-J7N18Y (store-layer-3) supports) — Line 4: one events file holds two lines with one id whose JSON encodings are equal but whose bytes differ; the store cut writes different bytes for the two line orders.
- R-SXCDZW#3 closed by R-RZMF87 (H-J7N18Y (store-layer-3) supports) — Line 5: the newest id is in the greatest ULID millisecond; an appended id is newest+1, the same on two clones, and the greatest ULID itself wraps to all zeros.
- R-RZMF87#1 closed by R-53N6JZ (H-DDJJY5 (store-layer-4) supports) — Line 5: the store holds an id above the greatest ULID, such as 80000000000000000000000000, which replay admits; an append succeeds with a clock id that sorts before it.
- R-53N6JZ#1 closed by R-YYCWTP (H-8QBZ4Q (store-layer-5) supports) — Line 5: an events file holds a line whose id replay rejects, such as the lower-case 01mzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzz, sorting after every applied id; an append writes an id that sorts before it.
- R-YYCWTP#1 closed by R-SJXBFW (H-T83XWY (store-layer-6) supports) — Line 5: no test shows that a rejected lower-case id line leaves the appended id in the clock's millisecond; the lower-case case of TestAppendAfterRejectedID also passes on ded3e4b.

## Q-D906CD What does a Result keep about the commits and files its cited runs were given? — answered

    - R-HW9B05 recorded H-Q7ZRZJ=supports H-X1Z61J=contradicts H-CXH63T=contradicts: S1 and S2 as argued in the session: one subject per run keeps both true; merged lists cover bare B through a patched run (S1); one base per Result leaves red on base with no single checked closer (S2). The owner chose one subject per run.
- H-X1Z61J One base per Result: a Result cites runs at one base only, beside the merged fingerprints — contradicted
- H-CXH63T Merged lists: a list of bases beside the one merged fingerprints list — contradicted

    - R-V8032Z recorded H-KVCHND=supports H-P5BA75=contradicts: S1: a Goal field goes wrong when the base moves; the runs' subjects stay true. S2: only builds would fill it, a delivery kind. ## Q-H8PJ20 What is the owner's act attached to when one store holds many Goals? — answered

Selected: H-J23DRM

- H-J23DRM To no node: one owner act may move keys of several Goals; each key leads to its Goal — supported
  - E-BC62A9 One owner sitting across two Goals decides what the act is attached to — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-8CJCV6 recorded H-J23DRM=supports H-379Z1S=contradicts: One sitting across two Goals is one act with no node; each key finds its Goal. Per-Goal acts split one sitting into several events. The owner chose no node; lines moved into the Goal's node.add, so the owner act never names a Goal.
- H-379Z1S To one Goal: each owner act names one Goal, so one sitting across Goals is several events — contradicted

## Q-R21S8R How many event types carry the owner's answers and the runner's checkpoints? — answered

Selected: H-H11D63

  - E-YNGH8F Whether the envelope rule can stay per type decides the number of types — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-AWPQ4X recorded H-H11D63=supports H-MATM0C=contradicts: The owner and runner acts differ in node and why, which the spec defines per type. Two types keep one envelope rule each and make field mixing a decode error; one type makes the envelope depend on the payload. The owner accepted two types, answer and checkpoint.
- H-MATM0C One type, checkpoint, its kind derived from its fields — contradicted

# G-73R704 Record the shared-store design: what hyp stores and derives when one store holds many Goals — complete

Design record for G1b (investigations/checkpoint-store), which builds it. Each question was settled in session e6a35a24 (2026-11-11) and is recorded as selected with that session as evidence, on the owner's decision. The decisions land in ADR 0020. No lines: no run checks a design argued in a session; G1b's lines carry the cases.

## Q-D906CD What does a Result keep about the commits and files its cited runs were given? — answered

Selected: H-Q7ZRZJ

- H-Q7ZRZJ One subject per cited run, {base, files}; a Hypothesis is covered when one entry holds its whole subject — supported
  - E-DA7082 Two scenarios argued in the session decide which shape keeps the claims true — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-HW9B05 recorded H-Q7ZRZJ=supports H-X1Z61J=contradicts H-CXH63T=contradicts: S1 and S2 as argued in the session: one subject per run keeps both true; merged lists cover bare B through a patched run (S1); one base per Result leaves red on base with no single checked closer (S2). The owner chose one subject per run.
- H-X1Z61J One base per Result: a Result cites runs at one base only, beside the merged fingerprints — contradicted
- H-CXH63T Merged lists: a list of bases beside the one merged fingerprints list — contradicted

## Q-HC4V9T Where is the commit a build Goal starts from recorded? — answered

Selected: H-KVCHND

- H-KVCHND In the subject of the red-on-base Result; the Goal records no commit — supported
  - E-WBQBPP Two scenarios argued in the session decide where the start commit lives — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-V8032Z recorded H-KVCHND=supports H-P5BA75=contradicts: S1: a Goal field goes wrong when the base moves; the runs' subjects stay true. S2: only builds would fill it, a delivery kind. The owner agreed the Goal records no start commit, and asked that a Goal's origin be known, which is derived from its cited keys.
- H-P5BA75 On the Goal: a start-commit field written when the Goal is added — contradicted

## Q-H8PJ20 What is the owner's act attached to when one store holds many Goals? — answered

Selected: H-J23DRM

- H-J23DRM To no node: one owner act may move keys of several Goals; each key leads to its Goal — supported
  - E-BC62A9 One owner sitting across two Goals decides what the act is attached to — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-8CJCV6 recorded H-J23DRM=supports H-379Z1S=contradicts: One sitting across two Goals is one act with no node; each key finds its Goal. Per-Goal acts split one sitting into several events. The owner chose no node; lines moved into the Goal's node.add, so the owner act never names a Goal.
- H-379Z1S To one Goal: each owner act names one Goal, so one sitting across Goals is several events — contradicted

## Q-R21S8R How many event types carry the owner's answers and the runner's checkpoints? — answered

Selected: H-H11D63

- H-H11D63 Two: answer (owner; no node, no why) and checkpoint (runner; node is the Goal, why required) — supported
  - E-YNGH8F Whether the envelope rule can stay per type decides the number of types — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-AWPQ4X recorded H-H11D63=supports H-MATM0C=contradicts: The owner and runner acts differ in node and why, which the spec defines per type. Two types keep one envelope rule each and make field mixing a decode error; one type makes the envelope depend on the payload. The owner accepted two types, answer and checkpoint.
- H-MATM0C One type, checkpoint, its kind derived from its fields — contradicted

### hyp render --markdown
# G-GZR6C6 Record how hyp makes the /goal prompt: who writes it, and whether it carries owner answers — complete

Design record for issue #113, settled by the owner in the grill session of 2026-11-11 (hyp-owner-grill). Two questions: who writes the /goal prompt for a Goal, hyp or a skill; and whether the prompt carries the owner's answers. Each is a DesignQuestion with its rivals, decided in that session with the session as evidence, so the tree says it was argued, not tested. This Goal has no lines and no run: no run checks its candidates. Its cases are the lines of the build Goal in this store that cites its selects.

## Q-NCY8HY Who writes the /goal prompt for a Goal: hyp from the log, or a skill? — answered

Issue #113: runs 1 to 6 of checkpoint-store:G-D1CH1R had hand-written /goal prompts. Scenario that separates the rivals: generate the prompt twice from a fixture of checkpoint-store:G-D1CH1R's log cut at the owner answer of 2026-11-11 14:31, and once in a store with a second Goal, then compare the outputs byte for byte and with run 6's prompt clause for clause.

Selected: H-MCDPNB
Design: A read command derives the prompt from the log: store path, actor, Goal id, the hyp-goal-run skill, the stop clauses and the Goal body's constraints block. hyp-owner-grill's hand-off and hyp-owner-answer's end call it. Same log and flags, same bytes.

- H-MCDPNB hyp prints it: hyp prompt <G-id> --actor A [--branch B] [--pr N] [--experiments N] — supported
  - E-VJPMHG The owner's scenarios in the grill session decide who writes the prompt — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-FCEZY6 recorded H-MCDPNB=supports H-E5K8VG=contradicts: S1 and S2 as argued in the session: a read command over the log gives the same bytes for the same log and flags and can refuse a missing heading; agent-written text cannot. The owner chose h-cmd.
- H-E5K8VG A skill writes it: hyp-owner-grill and hyp-owner-answer compose the prompt from hyp status and hyp show — contradicted

## Q-MAKJ8R Does the /goal prompt carry the owner's answers? — answered

Run 6's prompt opened with the owner's answer it started from. Scenarios that separate the rivals: (S1) hyp-owner-answer records an answer, then its own runner checkpoint, then the prompt is made; (S2) a run writes a refer-only checkpoint after an answer and dies before closing the returned limit; (S3) one answer event settles a key of each of two Goals; (S4) at the 14:31 cut of checkpoint-store:G-D1CH1R, OWED already lists the returned limit checkpoint-store:R-9SMRB2#4 for the runner.

Selected: H-RSDX0H
Design: The prompt holds no owner answer text and is byte-identical before and after an answer event.

- H-RSDX0H No answers clause: the runner reads OWED, which already lists each returned limit and each answered reading to apply — supported
  - E-T7ZS23 The owner's scenarios in the grill session decide whether the prompt carries answers — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-26GPDW recorded H-RSDX0H=supports H-9Y4F4H=contradicts H-CHKW8Y=contradicts: S1 to S4 as argued in the session: position-based answers vanish after hyp-owner-answer's own checkpoint; state-based answers repeat what OWED already lists. The owner chose h-none.
- H-9Y4F4H Answers from state: each returned, unclosed limit and each unapplied answered reading of this Goal — contradicted
- H-CHKW8Y Answers by position: the answer events after the Goal's latest runner checkpoint — contradicted

# G-ZBM2RE hyp prints the /goal prompt for a Goal from its log — complete

# G-908PRZ A budget-bound build run ends with its final candidate reviewed, without a review chain on small changes — complete

For issue #141 (part of #113). The candidate is a hyp commit over main 9c8e498b87683e50a94a410b6a3cfecdd7a0d441 that changes the run practice of a build Goal (review, budget, stop) in skills/hyp-goal-run, or hyp prompt's default N. Owner's prediction: H-whole-candidate is contradicted on cost.
Cut A: this store as of 01M75T1YRF2R2Z99DX0E8JQPWG (R-8JQPWG), actor claude-g113-1, code at b4f23b2. Cut B: as of 01M76V52SZ9DMCYFVYFRJEGDSA (run 2's stop), a fresh actor, code at 3cfd08d.
A trial replays a cut under the recipe the run builds, on Sonnet, with the method stated in the Question's body.
A review Result gives a verdict on a candidate and cites a review written by a subagent whose transcript in the trial read that candidate's diff.
May change: this repository, in commits on the branch the prompt names, in a worktree separate from the checkout ~/.claude/skills links to, following AGENTS.md; the run may push that branch and open or update one PR, and never merges it.
May change: skills/hyp-goal-run and the text the change makes wrong (skills/, docs/, AGENTS.md, README.md); cmd/, internal/ and testdata for H-raise-budget only; a new recipes/ directory.
Read only: plans/, every investigations/ tree except goal-prompt, ak-skill-creator under .claude/skills.
Never: commit to main or merge a PR.
Never: replace an installed hyp; compile each candidate to a separate path.

## Q-8YMFW1 Which run practice ends a budget-bound build run with its final candidate reviewed, without a review chain? — answered

Separating scenarios: replay cut A and cut B under each candidate. Method: sandbox at the cut's commit; the candidate's skills/ in wt/.claude/skills; claude -p on Sonnet with --setting-sources project; prompt printed in wt with N = the candidate's default; gh auth unset. Doctor: loaded hyp-goal-run sha256 = the candidate's; no later commit reachable. Same model, effort, claude version, timeout per trial. Rows (candidate, cut, trial) keep total_cost_usd, duration_ms, num_turns, pass; none dropped. Median: middle of 3 sorted costs; a trial with no result record counts highest.

Selected: H-SFEA2M
Design: The review of a candidate is recorded as its review Result: the subagent's reply saved word for word and cited by a Result that gives the candidate a verdict, inside its gate Experiment so it costs no extra Result. No select of a candidate without one; the stop checkpoint names each candidate that lacks one. Built as a commit that changes skills/hyp-goal-run. From R-4XNFHN: every arm asked subagents, but runs cited a summary or no Result.

Limits:

- R-S0BRXE#1 closed by R-T2WJ7V (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — whole-candidate F1, G-908PRZ#1: a run builds C1, fixes to C2 and C3, and reviews only C3, as 'one review replaces a review of each fix commit' says; C1 and C2 end with no review Result.
- R-S0BRXE#2 accepted by owner-dat — whole-candidate F2, G-908PRZ#4: the review is tied to the select; a run that stops on its budget with no select owed asks for no review, and nothing names the unreviewed candidate.
- R-S0BRXE#3 accepted by owner-dat — name-at-stop F2, G-908PRZ#4: with many unreviewed candidates and AWAITING OWNER keys, the stop summary passes 600 runes; hyp refuses it, or the run trims the unreviewed names.
- R-S0BRXE#4 accepted by owner-dat — reserve-last F3, G-908PRZ#1: the rule fires only at exactly one Result left; Results spent on readings or limit closes leave zero at the next commit, which then has no review Result.
- R-S0BRXE#5 closed by R-T2WJ7V (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — one-experiment F1, G-908PRZ#4: the gate-plus-review Result of C1 finds a text defect at the budget's end; the fix C2 is committed with no Result left and the stop checkpoint does not name it.
- R-S0BRXE#6 closed by R-T2WJ7V (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — one-experiment F3, G-908PRZ#1: a review with cases of lines adds limit-closing Results, so a candidate costs more than one Result and the budget can end before the select.
- R-S0BRXE#7 accepted by owner-dat — raise-budget F1, G-908PRZ#4: with skill text unchanged, readings, limit closes and reassess Results fill all 7 before the review; the run stops on an unreviewed candidate it does not name.
- R-S0BRXE#8 accepted by owner-dat — raise-budget F3, G-908PRZ#3: a run that stopped at its 5th Result can record two more before the stop clause, so its median cost can pass 1.5 times the base at N 5.
- R-S0BRXE#9 accepted by owner-dat — probe-closes F4, G-908PRZ#1: k probes run before the review Result is recorded; with N 5 the budget ends before the review Result exists.
- R-S0BRXE#10 accepted by owner-dat — probe-closes F2, G-908PRZ#1: one passing probe of a narrow finding is recorded as --closes on a whole Goal line, which no replay of that line ran.
- R-4XNFHN#1 accepted by owner-dat — G-908PRZ#1 and #2: score reads 'a review written by a subagent' as a cited file holding the subagent's own text. No check ran the reading 'the runner's summary of that review'; by results-and-selects.txt it would not lift any candidate over all four lines.
- R-4XNFHN#2 closed by R-BHPHN5 (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — G-908PRZ#3: total_cost_usd is the session's cost-state total; whether it holds the subagents' own cost was not checked, so an arm that spends more on reviews may be under-counted.
- R-KWYBA6#1 closed by R-RSV3E7 (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — G-908PRZ#1, #2: the text names git show <commit>, the delta against the parent, not git diff <base> <commit>; a fix commit's review misses defects carried from the commit before it.
- R-KWYBA6#2 closed by R-RSV3E7 (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — G-908PRZ#1, #3: the text does not order the review before the gate's Result is recorded; a run that records the gate first spends a second Result on the review.
- R-KWYBA6#3 closed by R-RSV3E7 (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — G-908PRZ#1: the pre-registered verdict rule does not name the review or what its findings do to the verdict.
- R-KWYBA6#4 closed by R-RSV3E7 (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — G-908PRZ#3: each fix commit is a new candidate and gets its own review, a review chain on small changes; no text lets a fix reuse its predecessor's review.
- R-KWYBA6#5 closed by R-RSV3E7 (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — G-908PRZ#1: a candidate committed after the last Result is used is built but never gated, so never reviewed; the text has no rule against it.
- R-KWYBA6#6 closed by R-RSV3E7 (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — G-908PRZ#1, #2: 'Review each candidate before any select of it' reads as every candidate or only a candidate to be selected.
- R-KWYBA6#7 closed by R-RSV3E7 (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — G-908PRZ#1, #2: 'Give it the code' lets a runner paste a summary, so the subagent's transcript may not hold the diff.
- R-YNB43F#1 accepted by owner-dat — G-908PRZ#3: the base rows are E-XVFAZ6's trials from earlier the same day, not run in the same batch as this arm; same model and claude version, but not interleaved.
- R-YNB43F#2 closed by R-BHPHN5 (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — G-908PRZ#3: total_cost_usd is the session's cost-state total; whether it holds the subagents' own cost was not checked, so this arm's review cost may be under-counted.

Design record for issue #113, settled by the owner in the grill session of 2026-11-11 (hyp-owner-grill). Two questions: who writes the /goal prompt for a Goal, hyp or a skill; and whether the prompt carries the owner's answers. Each is a DesignQuestion with its rivals, decided in that session with the session as evidence, so the tree says it was argued, not tested. This Goal has no lines and no run: no run checks its candidates. Its cases are the lines of the build Goal in this store that cites its selects.

## Q-NCY8HY Who writes the /goal prompt for a Goal: hyp from the log, or a skill? — answered

Issue #113: runs 1 to 6 of checkpoint-store:G-D1CH1R had hand-written /goal prompts. Scenario that separates the rivals: generate the prompt twice from a fixture of checkpoint-store:G-D1CH1R's log cut at the owner answer of 2026-11-11 14:31, and once in a store with a second Goal, then compare the outputs byte for byte and with run 6's prompt clause for clause.

Selected: H-MCDPNB
Design: A read command derives the prompt from the log: store path, actor, Goal id, the hyp-goal-run skill, the stop clauses and the Goal body's constraints block. hyp-owner-grill's hand-off and hyp-owner-answer's end call it. Same log and flags, same bytes.

- H-MCDPNB hyp prints it: hyp prompt <G-id> --actor A [--branch B] [--pr N] [--experiments N] — supported
  - E-VJPMHG The owner's scenarios in the grill session decide who writes the prompt — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-FCEZY6 recorded H-MCDPNB=supports H-E5K8VG=contradicts: S1 and S2 as argued in the session: a read command over the log gives the same bytes for the same log and flags and can refuse a missing heading; agent-written text cannot. The owner chose h-cmd.
- H-E5K8VG A skill writes it: hyp-owner-grill and hyp-owner-answer compose the prompt from hyp status and hyp show — contradicted

## Q-MAKJ8R Does the /goal prompt carry the owner's answers? — answered

Run 6's prompt opened with the owner's answer it started from. Scenarios that separate the rivals: (S1) hyp-owner-answer records an answer, then its own runner checkpoint, then the prompt is made; (S2) a run writes a refer-only checkpoint after an answer and dies before closing the returned limit; (S3) one answer event settles a key of each of two Goals; (S4) at the 14:31 cut of checkpoint-store:G-D1CH1R, OWED already lists the returned limit checkpoint-store:R-9SMRB2#4 for the runner.

Selected: H-RSDX0H
Design: The prompt holds no owner answer text and is byte-identical before and after an answer event.

## Q-8YMFW1 Which run practice ends a budget-bound build run with its final candidate reviewed, without a review chain? — answered

Separating scenarios: replay cut A and cut B under each candidate. Method: sandbox at the cut's commit; the candidate's skills/ in wt/.claude/skills; claude -p on Sonnet with --setting-sources project; prompt printed in wt with N = the candidate's default; gh auth unset. Doctor: loaded hyp-goal-run sha256 = the candidate's; no later commit reachable. Same model, effort, claude version, timeout per trial. Rows (candidate, cut, trial) keep total_cost_usd, duration_ms, num_turns, pass; none dropped. Median: middle of 3 sorted costs; a trial with no result record counts highest.

Selected: H-SFEA2M
Design: The review of a candidate is recorded as its review Result: the subagent's reply saved word for word and cited by a Result that gives the candidate a verdict, inside its gate Experiment so it costs no extra Result. No select of a candidate without one; the stop checkpoint names each candidate that lacks one. Built as a commit that changes skills/hyp-goal-run. From R-4XNFHN: every arm asked subagents, but runs cited a summary or no Result.

Limits:

- R-S0BRXE#1 closed by R-T2WJ7V (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — whole-candidate F1, G-908PRZ#1: a run builds C1, fixes to C2 and C3, and reviews only C3, as 'one review replaces a review of each fix commit' says; C1 and C2 end with no review Result.
- R-S0BRXE#2 accepted by owner-dat — whole-candidate F2, G-908PRZ#4: the review is tied to the select; a run that stops on its budget with no select owed asks for no review, and nothing names the unreviewed candidate.
- R-S0BRXE#3 accepted by owner-dat — name-at-stop F2, G-908PRZ#4: with many unreviewed candidates and AWAITING OWNER keys, the stop summary passes 600 runes; hyp refuses it, or the run trims the unreviewed names.
- R-S0BRXE#4 accepted by owner-dat — reserve-last F3, G-908PRZ#1: the rule fires only at exactly one Result left; Results spent on readings or limit closes leave zero at the next commit, which then has no review Result.
- R-S0BRXE#5 closed by R-T2WJ7V (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — one-experiment F1, G-908PRZ#4: the gate-plus-review Result of C1 finds a text defect at the budget's end; the fix C2 is committed with no Result left and the stop checkpoint does not name it.
- R-S0BRXE#6 closed by R-T2WJ7V (H-SFEA2M (review-result) supports) — one-experiment F3, G-908PRZ#1: a review with cases of lines adds limit-closing Results, so a candidate costs more than one Result and the budget can end before the select.
- R-S0BRXE#7 accepted by owner-dat — raise-budget F1, G-908PRZ#4: with skill text unchanged, readings, limit closes and reassess Results fill all 7 before the review; the run stops on an unreviewed candidate it does not name.
- R-S0BRXE#8 accepted by owner-dat — raise-budget F3, G-908PRZ#3: a run that stopped at its 5th Result can record two more before the stop clause, so its median cost can pass 1.5 times the base at N 5.

# G-4S36M5 A write command never appends text that cites a node id this store does not hold, without the operator being told — complete

## Q-DVZZHY Which rule stops a write command from appending text that cites an id that is no node? — answered

Selected: H-88ZF3E
Design: Every write command scans --why, --body, --observation, --summary and each --limit for id-shaped tokens: at a word boundary, G-, Q-, H-, E- or R- (upper case) then six Crockford-base32 upper-case characters, or a placeholder mark (<, {, …, ..., or three or more X). Lower-case prose like E-mail is never id-shaped. A token not preceded by <name>: that does not resolve in this store refuses: nothing is appended and hyp exits 2, as for unknown node.

Limits:

- R-BV4CWV#1 accepted by owner-dat — The fixture is hand-made apart from K1, and small: one store, and request-verify for most cases.
- R-BV4CWV#2 accepted by owner-dat — K7 is unscored for the scan arms: a scan cannot see an id that is not written.
- R-BV4CWV#3 accepted by owner-dat — The arms are prototypes; the delivered rule must be reproduced in Go and give the selected arm's rows on every case.
- R-BV4CWV#4 accepted by owner-dat — h-cite's cases run request-verify only; select and confirm take --result in the arm but no case runs them. Under h-cite the why still holds R-<new> (C1a).
- R-BV4CWV#5 accepted by owner-dat — The arm binaries are read from absolute paths outside the bundle, which the known-answer gate cannot see.

- H-RAJAFK Scan text flags for id-shaped tokens; warn on an unresolved one and still append — supported
- H-88ZF3E Scan text flags for id-shaped tokens; refuse an unresolved one — supported
  - E-YKJ70D Each rule's arm on the frozen fixture: exit, append and warning per case — run (recipe id-citation-arms@1, run and rule)
    - R-BV4CWV recorded H-RAJAFK=supports H-88ZF3E=supports H-C5BA8J=supports H-BMN916=contradicts: id-citation-arms@1 cases on the frozen fixture: the today arm appended every slip K1-K6 (K1 is the rebuilt event) with exit 0 and no warning. text-warn exited 0, appended and warned on K1-K6; text-refuse exited 2 and appended nothing on K1-K6; both gave exit 0, appended, no warning on G1-G5. cite refused C1a and C1b (exit 2, nothing appended), accepted C1c, and matched today on G1-G5. K7 (prose) passed every arm unflagged, unscored. verdict printed supports for three rivals: an owner trade-off.
- H-C5BA8J request-verify, select and confirm take --result REF, resolved or refused — supported
- H-BMN916 No change: today's hyp is enough — contradicted
exit 0

### hyp render --markdown
# G-5W1F5S An open reading holds its question's decision, and the next Hypothesis while the work rests on it — complete

The owner's rule for #174 (2026-11-14): a reading holds the decision, not the work. An open reading holds its question's select, choose and reaffirm, whatever its reach. It holds the question's next Hypothesis while its answer can move a live Hypothesis there, or while no open limit that a Result on the question recorded is unreferred, and it holds the undecided item of a Hypothesis its Result judged inconclusive. Holding the decision does not mean the runner builds on a guess while a line is unclear. Replay derives every hold from the log; nothing new is stored.

Event: in run 4 of record:G-SZCBXZ, record:R-V2CMZQ supported h-record-9 and recorded reading record:R-V2CMZQ#r1. The runner then added h-record-10b while OWED owed it nothing. The supersede left the reading with an empty reach, so it held nothing.

Words: Q is a DesignQuestion. A Result on Q is a non-retracted Result whose Experiment's nearest question is Q, and a reading on Q is a reading of a Result on Q. A Hypothesis a reading's answer can move is a live one whose parent is Q, that the reading's Result judged, and that is supported now, inconclusive now when that Result's verdict on it was inconclusive, whatever other Results judged (line 2), or contradicted with that Result as its only contradiction.

The Goal does not ask about an answered question: an open reading changes no question's state and no Goal's state. It does not ask about hyp retract: a retracted Result's readings hold nothing. Lines 4 and 5 hold on the base, commit afe2c1f1e9cad835edb755b6c08deaae279afd1b.

The same PR updates docs/replay-spec.md (section 4 readings, 5, 5.1, 7.3 and 8.1), docs/glossary.md (the rivals, undecided and stale rows, and a new entry for reaffirm), docs/owner-guide.md, skills/hyp-prototype and skills/hyp-goal-run, which no longer copies a reading onto a later Result by hand.

May change: this repository, by commits on one local branch feat/reading-hold and a PR the run never merges
May change: internal/, cmd/, docs/ and skills/
Read only: every other store under investigations/
Never: replace the installed hyp binary

## Q-F0E223 Which hyp commit implements the hold rule? — answered

Each Hypothesis is one commit on feat/reading-hold. Its rival is the base afe2c1f1e9cad835edb755b6c08deaae279afd1b: each new test of lines 1, 2, 3 and 6 is red on the base and green on the candidate, and the tests of lines 4 and 5 are green on both.

Selected: H-MEHPH5
Design: Successor of h-hold-1 (33f18e0). Commit 29f27cb adds admitSupersede in internal/cli/write.go, called by cmdSupersede and by cmdLink for a supersedes edge.add, so every command that writes the same supersedes edge exits 1 while an open reading's answer can move H (answer R-99GH9F#r1, limit R-KKRB2S#1). It adds TestLinkSupersedesHeldLikeSupersede and updates docs/replay-spec.md §4 readings and §5.1 and both skills.

Limits:

- R-KKRB2S#1 closed by R-M6Q1TY (H-MEHPH5 (h-hold-2) supports) — hyp link NEW supersedes H while an open reading on H's question can move H (line G-5W1F5S#3, answer R-99GH9F#r1)

### hyp render --markdown
# G-5W1F5S An open reading holds its question's decision, and the next Hypothesis while the work rests on it — complete

The owner's rule for #174 (2026-11-14): a reading holds the decision, not the work. An open reading holds its question's select, choose and reaffirm, whatever its reach. It holds the question's next Hypothesis while its answer can move a live Hypothesis there, or while no open limit that a Result on the question recorded is unreferred, and it holds the undecided item of a Hypothesis its Result judged inconclusive. Holding the decision does not mean the runner builds on a guess while a line is unclear. Replay derives every hold from the log; nothing new is stored.

Event: in run 4 of record:G-SZCBXZ, record:R-V2CMZQ supported h-record-9 and recorded reading record:R-V2CMZQ#r1. The runner then added h-record-10b while OWED owed it nothing. The supersede left the reading with an empty reach, so it held nothing.

Words: Q is a DesignQuestion. A Result on Q is a non-retracted Result whose Experiment's nearest question is Q, and a reading on Q is a reading of a Result on Q. A Hypothesis a reading's answer can move is a live one whose parent is Q, that the reading's Result judged, and that is supported now, inconclusive now when that Result's verdict on it was inconclusive, whatever other Results judged (line 2), or contradicted with that Result as its only contradiction.

The Goal does not ask about an answered question: an open reading changes no question's state and no Goal's state. It does not ask about hyp retract: a retracted Result's readings hold nothing. Lines 4 and 5 hold on the base, commit afe2c1f1e9cad835edb755b6c08deaae279afd1b.

The same PR updates docs/replay-spec.md (section 4 readings, 5, 5.1, 7.3 and 8.1), docs/glossary.md (the rivals, undecided and stale rows, and a new entry for reaffirm), docs/owner-guide.md, skills/hyp-prototype and skills/hyp-goal-run, which no longer copies a reading onto a later Result by hand.

May change: this repository, by commits on one local branch feat/reading-hold and a PR the run never merges
May change: internal/, cmd/, docs/ and skills/
Read only: every other store under investigations/
Never: replace the installed hyp binary

### hyp render --markdown
# G-93VFSB A first author with only hyp and its skills builds a working runner-gate instrument with fewer stops given a base — complete

## Q-WXZRA3 Does an embedded base help a first author build a runner instrument, and if so, how is the base split? — answered

Selected: H-HYXCF9
Design: Read over the held-out recipe as the mean over trials per arm: neither base arm has pass count >= null's and mean stops <= null's mean stops - 1. Today's path (recipe-template.md + RECIPE-FORMAT.md) is the arm; the null hyp binary embeds no base.

Limits:

- R-H5H95P#1 accepted by owner-dat — Both base-arm end-state fails (coarse-3, fine-5) and their f_weak_cases stops come from one base defect: score-usage's empty input/, which remove and git drop (empty-input.txt). Fixed, coarse is 5/5 at 0.80 and fine 5/5 at 0.40: gaps 0.40 and 0.80, still under the 1-stop bar.
- R-H5H95P#2 accepted by owner-dat — The rule's bar counts stops, not writing: bases cut edited lines from 1068 to about 230 per trial, but null runners also passed 5/5 at 1.2 stops, so the measured benefit is less writing, which the rule does not reward.
- R-H5H95P#3 accepted by owner-dat — One method (the skill-text gate), five sonnet trials per arm, candidates derived from three recipes this repository wrote; two null runners made and discarded nested smoke sandboxes in the operator's ~/.hyp-sandbox.

- H-HYXCF9 Template and RECIPE-FORMAT are enough: a base gives no fewer stops, no better end state on held-out skill-text-arms — supported
  - E-3S24B5 Sonnet runners build the held-out skill-text gate instrument on null, coarse and fine arms; stops, end state, edits — run (recipe recipe-base-arms@1, run and rule)
    - R-H5H95P recorded H-HYXCF9=supports H-BFRAPB=contradicts H-Z3PBEA=contradicts: verdict (recipe-base-arms@1, 5 trials/arm after the pre-registered extension at 3): null mean stops 1.20, pass 5/5, edited 1068; coarse 1.00, 4/5, 217; fine 0.60, 4/5, 249. No base arm beats null (pass not worse and stops at least 1 lower), so h-null supports. Every base runner (10/10) started from its bases and wrote about a fifth of null's lines. Runners: sonnet, USD 11.88 total.
- H-BFRAPB One base:claude-runner-gate@1 (Launch, Doctor, Drive, Cleanup, score, verdict) gives the fewest stops and edits — contradicted
- H-Z3PBEA Bases claude-runner, transcript-score, gate-verdict and fixture-check give the fewest stops and edits — contradicted
exit 0

### hyp render --markdown
# G-4N85GM Record where hyp keeps the record off every code branch — complete

Part of #181, order 1 of #180. A design record: its one question was settled by argument in the grill of 2026-11-14, with the session as evidence, so it has no lines; its cases go to the lines of the build Goal that follows it in this store. A settled shape, not a question: the ref is hidden (refs/hyp/record), not a branch, so no GitHub button deletes, merges or checks it out, and a plain git fetch does not bring it; hyp sync does.

## Q-9WV3D7 Where does the record live, off every code branch? — answered

On a crash after an event's line is in the record worktree's file and before its commit, h-ref meets the build Goal's line 3 (no command reads part of a write) and h-record-worktree does not, because a reader of that worktree sees an event that git clean or git checkout there can still lose, while under h-ref a reader sees the event only once update-ref has landed. If h-record-worktree read only commits, it would be h-ref plus a checkout that adds nothing.

Selected: H-WQ2Q24
Design: hyp writes each event, and the bytes of each file it names, as git objects, then moves refs/hyp/record to a new commit with update-ref; an event exists for every reader of the clone only once the ref has moved, and no worktree holds the record's files.

- H-WQ2Q24 The record is commits on a hidden ref, written by git plumbing and never checked out — supported
  - E-DETSNS Settled in the grill: the crash between a record worktree's file line and its commit — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-YK6G15 recorded H-WQ2Q24=supports H-P8A9N7=contradicts: Settled by argument in the grill of 2026-11-14 with the owner: on a crash between a record worktree's file line and its commit, h-record-worktree lets a reader see an event a later git act on the worktree loses; h-ref shows an event only once update-ref lands.
- H-P8A9N7 The record is a branch holding only .tree/, checked out in its own worktree — contradicted

# G-SZCBXZ hyp keeps the record apart from every code branch, owned by one module: the log, the files it names and the commits it cites — in_progress

Part of #180 (order 1); builds #181. Covers #179, #152, #177's landing step and part of #151; it decides the record for #168, whose filters (status --all, cites across stores, alias scope) are a later Goal. The candidate is a hyp commit; go test on its base and on the candidate is the instrument.

The record: every event of every store of one git repository, the bytes of every file an event names (evidence, an Experiment's records, a subject's patch and files, also of retracted Results), each bundle an event pins, and every commit a Hypothesis subject names, kept by hyp as commits on the hidden ref refs/hyp/record, which no branch holds. A record repository is a git repository that holds that ref.

Decided in the grill of 2026-11-14 (design record record-design in this store, h-ref selected): a write is durable once it exits 0; one record per repository, and a store keeps its name, its folder's path from the repository root; hyp sync [REMOTE] fetches the remote's record, joins the two logs as a set by id and pushes, and the hyp-goal-run skill runs it at every stop; the record keeps only cited files, and git ignores every other file under .tree/evidence/ (#152); an evidence pointer stores its file's sha256; a sandbox clone, a git repository with no record ref and a directory with no git use the work-tree .tree/ as today; hyp import [REF:]PATH... names its stores and may run again. After this Goal merges, the owner imports this repository's five stores and tag g-akjjf8/record; old .tree/ folders stay as frozen copies, cited files tracked. Accepted: a hyp built before this change reads only those copies, sees no later event, and rejects an event that stores an evidence sha256. Reopens ADR 0022 and 0023 (unmerged); a new ADR records it; the glossary gains Record and Record repository.

Each Experiment's rubric also runs hyp import in a sandbox clone of this repository and compares each Goal's node statuses with its work-tree store's.

Line 3 and the last sentence of line 9 hold on the base.

May change: hyp in this repository (cmd/, internal/, docs/), as commits on one local branch pushed as a PR the run never merges
May change: any text in skills/, README.md or AGENTS.md the change makes wrong
May change: this Goal's store, investigations/record, written with the installed hyp on a branch cut from main that holds only .tree/ changes, in its own worktree, pushed as a record PR the owner merges at every stop
Read only: the events and evidence of every other store under investigations/; every frozen recipe version under recipes/ and investigations/*/recipes/; the tags g-akjjf8/record, g-akjjf8/candidates and goal-prompt/subject-*
Never: run a candidate's hyp so that it writes a ref, a record or a store in this clone or on origin; its tests and Experiments use fixtures, temporary repositories and sandbox clones
Never: replace the installed hyp binary at ~/.local/bin/hyp
Never: edit or remove a line of any events file
Never: merge a PR

# G-BPWZ24 hyp keeps the record apart from every code branch: one history of every store, by name and id — in_progress

Part of #180 (order 2); builds #181. It replaces build Goal G-SZCBXZ in this store, whose lines 1, 5, 8 and 9 left their range open, so each review in runs 4 and 5 found a new case. G-SZCBXZ stays in_progress, and no run acts on it.

The record is that of G-SZCBXZ's body and design record G-4N85GM (h-ref selected): every event of every store of one git repository, the bytes of every file an event names, each bundle an event pins, and every commit a subject names, kept as commits on the hidden ref refs/hyp/record. A record repository is a git repository that holds that ref. The record is the one history of #168: every event once, by an id unique across the repository. Its filters, the owner's overview and the relations between Goals, are a later Goal.

The owner decided in this grill on 2026-11-14: a store's name is its folder's resolved path, and a store folder's name is plain; every file path keeps any path git can hold; hyp import adds each event once, by id, with its bytes or not at all; a read during hyp's own snapshot prune is covered.

The base is commit c5b34da6e8eea5b507bed00d624b46afddbe4a44 (h-record-16 of G-SZCBXZ) on feat/record. Its tests that a later import adds what an earlier import lacked go with the first candidate. Lines 5 and 7 hold on the base. Line 1 covers that an event's files survive in one clone, not how two contents at one path show after a sync. A store folder moved or renamed after its events are in the record is not a case. Line 8 does not cover other bytes at the path of a file an event names, and a store with a named file, bundle or subject commit missing from every source is not a case. The run continues on feat/record and PR #187, merges main into it, from 7237c49 with #174's hold rule on, and never rebases it, and gives each Hypothesis an alias that starts with h-rec2-.

Each Experiment's rubric also runs hyp import in a temporary clone of this repository and compares each Goal's node statuses with its work-tree store's.

May change: hyp in this repository (cmd/, internal/, docs/), as commits on the local branch feat/record pushed to PR #187, which the run never merges

May change: this Goal's store, investigations/record, written with the installed hyp on a branch cut from main that holds only .tree/ changes, in its own worktree, pushed as a record PR the owner merges at every stop
Read only: the events and evidence of every other store under investigations/; every frozen recipe version under recipes/ and investigations/*/recipes/; the tags g-akjjf8/record, g-akjjf8/candidates and goal-prompt/subject-*

### hyp render --markdown
# G-4N85GM Record where hyp keeps the record off every code branch — complete

Part of #181, order 1 of #180. A design record: its one question was settled by argument in the grill of 2026-11-14, with the session as evidence, so it has no lines; its cases go to the lines of the build Goal that follows it in this store. A settled shape, not a question: the ref is hidden (refs/hyp/record), not a branch, so no GitHub button deletes, merges or checks it out, and a plain git fetch does not bring it; hyp sync does.

## Q-9WV3D7 Where does the record live, off every code branch? — answered

On a crash after an event's line is in the record worktree's file and before its commit, h-ref meets the build Goal's line 3 (no command reads part of a write) and h-record-worktree does not, because a reader of that worktree sees an event that git clean or git checkout there can still lose, while under h-ref a reader sees the event only once update-ref has landed. If h-record-worktree read only commits, it would be h-ref plus a checkout that adds nothing.

Selected: H-WQ2Q24
Design: hyp writes each event, and the bytes of each file it names, as git objects, then moves refs/hyp/record to a new commit with update-ref; an event exists for every reader of the clone only once the ref has moved, and no worktree holds the record's files.

- H-WQ2Q24 The record is commits on a hidden ref, written by git plumbing and never checked out — supported
  - E-DETSNS Settled in the grill: the crash between a record worktree's file line and its commit — run (recipe none, rule by reading)
    - R-YK6G15 recorded H-WQ2Q24=supports H-P8A9N7=contradicts: Settled by argument in the grill of 2026-11-14 with the owner: on a crash between a record worktree's file line and its commit, h-record-worktree lets a reader see an event a later git act on the worktree loses; h-ref shows an event only once update-ref lands.
- H-P8A9N7 The record is a branch holding only .tree/, checked out in its own worktree — contradicted

# G-SZCBXZ hyp keeps the record apart from every code branch, owned by one module: the log, the files it names and the commits it cites — in_progress

Part of #180 (order 1); builds #181. Covers #179, #152, #177's landing step and part of #151; it decides the record for #168, whose filters (status --all, cites across stores, alias scope) are a later Goal. The candidate is a hyp commit; go test on its base and on the candidate is the instrument.

The record: every event of every store of one git repository, the bytes of every file an event names (evidence, an Experiment's records, a subject's patch and files, also of retracted Results), each bundle an event pins, and every commit a Hypothesis subject names, kept by hyp as commits on the hidden ref refs/hyp/record, which no branch holds. A record repository is a git repository that holds that ref.

Decided in the grill of 2026-11-14 (design record record-design in this store, h-ref selected): a write is durable once it exits 0; one record per repository, and a store keeps its name, its folder's path from the repository root; hyp sync [REMOTE] fetches the remote's record, joins the two logs as a set by id and pushes, and the hyp-goal-run skill runs it at every stop; the record keeps only cited files, and git ignores every other file under .tree/evidence/ (#152); an evidence pointer stores its file's sha256; a sandbox clone, a git repository with no record ref and a directory with no git use the work-tree .tree/ as today; hyp import [REF:]PATH... names its stores and may run again. After this Goal merges, the owner imports this repository's five stores and tag g-akjjf8/record; old .tree/ folders stay as frozen copies, cited files tracked. Accepted: a hyp built before this change reads only those copies, sees no later event, and rejects an event that stores an evidence sha256. Reopens ADR 0022 and 0023 (unmerged); a new ADR records it; the glossary gains Record and Record repository.

Each Experiment's rubric also runs hyp import in a sandbox clone of this repository and compares each Goal's node statuses with its work-tree store's.

Line 3 and the last sentence of line 9 hold on the base.

# G-BPWZ24 hyp keeps the record apart from every code branch: one history of every store, by name and id — in_progress

Part of #180 (order 2); builds #181. It replaces build Goal G-SZCBXZ in this store, whose lines 1, 5, 8 and 9 left their range open, so each review in runs 4 and 5 found a new case. G-SZCBXZ stays in_progress, and no run acts on it.

The record is that of G-SZCBXZ's body and design record G-4N85GM (h-ref selected): every event of every store of one git repository, the bytes of every file an event names, each bundle an event pins, and every commit a subject names, kept as commits on the hidden ref refs/hyp/record. A record repository is a git repository that holds that ref. The record is the one history of #168: every event once, by an id unique across the repository. Its filters, the owner's overview and the relations between Goals, are a later Goal.

The owner decided in this grill on 2026-11-14: a store's name is its folder's resolved path, and a store folder's name is plain; every file path keeps any path git can hold; hyp import adds each event once, by id, with its bytes or not at all; a read during hyp's own snapshot prune is covered.

The base is commit c5b34da6e8eea5b507bed00d624b46afddbe4a44 (h-record-16 of G-SZCBXZ) on feat/record. Its tests that a later import adds what an earlier import lacked go with the first candidate. Lines 5 and 7 hold on the base. Line 1 covers that an event's files survive in one clone, not how two contents at one path show after a sync. A store folder moved or renamed after its events are in the record is not a case. Line 8 does not cover other bytes at the path of a file an event names, and a store with a named file, bundle or subject commit missing from every source is not a case. The run continues on feat/record and PR #187, merges main into it, from 7237c49 with #174's hold rule on, and never rebases it, and gives each Hypothesis an alias that starts with h-rec2-.

Each Experiment's rubric also runs hyp import in a temporary clone of this repository and compares each Goal's node statuses with its work-tree store's.

May change: hyp in this repository (cmd/, internal/, docs/), as commits on the local branch feat/record pushed to PR #187, which the run never merges
May change: any text in skills/, README.md or AGENTS.md the change makes wrong
May change: this Goal's store, investigations/record, written with the installed hyp on a branch cut from main that holds only .tree/ changes, in its own worktree, pushed as a record PR the owner merges at every stop
Read only: the events and evidence of every other store under investigations/; every frozen recipe version under recipes/ and investigations/*/recipes/; the tags g-akjjf8/record, g-akjjf8/candidates and goal-prompt/subject-*
Never: run a candidate's hyp so that it writes a ref, a record or a store in this clone or on origin; its tests and Experiments use fixtures, temporary repositories and sandbox clones
Never: replace the installed hyp binary at ~/.local/bin/hyp
Never: edit or remove a line of any events file
Never: merge a PR
